Sources: `scheduler.ts`, `scheduler-store.ts`, `lib/shared/schedule-skip.ts`, `lib/shared/schedule-form.ts` and the schedule routes. Normalize the run `pid` in the recorders, then re-record the scheduler, schedule-routes and schedule-stream goldens. deps: G07c
  — done 2026-11-15 (background agent): R01 + G07d's Rust half. No new route.
    - 2.1.302: new `claudius_core::schedule_skip` (the reason codes, `run_note_text`).
      - `record_skip` writes the skipped run and stamps `lastSkipReason` / `lastSkipAt`; a started run clears them.
      - A cwd that isn't a directory skips as `workspace_folder_missing` (in flight while it's checked), and notifies once per new reason.
      - Runs carry `pid`.
      - `boot()` runs `recover_interrupted_runs` before arming. An owner-gone `running` run becomes `error` / `interrupted` with no `endedAt`, then `lastStatus: "error"` if it was the latest run and the job isn't in flight. Owner-gone means not live here, not our pid, and `kill(pid,0)` failing other than EPERM; or, unstamped, a JS `startedAt < processStartedAt`.
      - A failed boot is kept and rethrown (`bootPromise`).
      - No `result` gives `session_ended_without_result`, and the bus gets `runNoteText(note)`.
      - A PATCH with `cwd` / `enabled` clears the skip pair.
    - 2.1.312: new `claudius_core::schedule_form`. POST/PATCH `effort`: a level is stored, `null` / `""` clears it, anything else is 400 `unknown effort` (after the cron's 400, before the cwd's). A truthy job `effort` goes into the run's options. `scheduler-store.ts` changed types only.
    - ecab6369: `session-loops/cancel` is 410 for a dead session. It uses R03's `Session::is_dead`; R01's own copy was dropped at the merge.
    - G07d: the scheduler is on by default. `--no-scheduler` or `CLAUDIUS_SCHEDULER=0|false|no|off` (trimmed, any case) turns it off; `--scheduler` forces it on. `main` wires `scheduler::record_on_bus`: it was never wired, and without it the inbox would lose scheduled runs once Next's scheduler is off.
    - Differences:

- [x] **R03** Dead-session restart: 464b3dfe, 7e183a25, ecab6369 and 7d75710b. Sources: `session-manager.ts`, `session-resume.ts`, the `input`, `stream`, `queue/send-all` and `queue/[uuid]/send-now` routes, `session.ts`, `events.ts`.
  — done 2026-11-15 (background agent): R03 dead-session restart (CC 2.1.311; TS 464b3dfe, 7e183a25, ecab6369 minus its ultrathink and loop-cancel parts, 7d75710b).
    - `Session::{is_dead, came_up_live, close_streams}`. Dead means done and either died before `end()` or wasn't aborted; `came_up_live` means a live `system/init` arrived.
      - `has_armed_loops` is false once done, and `send_queued_now` leaves the row queued once done.
      - The stream-error and spawn-failure `error` events carry `sessionEnded: true`.
    - `SessionManager::restart_if_dead(id, passive)` rebuilds under the same id from `rebuild_options`.
      - Concurrent callers share one rebuild. At most 3 run in a row; the budget refills after 5 min or after a successful turn (`note_thinking_recovery_success`).
      - Passive callers (`get_or_resume`: stream, pollers) get only the first attempt, and only after a live init.
      - A failed rebuild re-registers the dead instance through the shared `register()` and keeps its streams.
      - `dead_session_response`: 404 gone, 503 max_attempts, 503 "Couldn't restart the session: …" (`unknown error` if there is none).
    - Routes:
      - `POST /input` now reads the body as `req.json().catch(() => null)` and validates it before any restart: an empty, malformed or falsy body gets 400 on any id. It drops empty `inlinePastes` entries.
      - `/input`, `queue/[uuid]/send-now` and `queue/send-all` all go through `restart_if_dead`.
    - Deliberate differences:

      - `close_streams` ends every subscriber; the stream route's are the only kind.

The files route now answers a known binary extension from `stat` alone — **before** the 2 MiB cap, so a real `.parquet` / `.heic` gets the placeholder rather than a 413 — and anything that NUL-sniffs as binary gets `{content: "", binary: true, encoding: "unsupported", encodingLabel: "binary", editable: false, readOnlyReason: "Binary file — not shown as text."}`. `?serve=1` still wins above it (415 for a non-image extension), and the extension is lowercased by JS `toLowerCase`, so a Kelvin-sign `MKV` counts.
    - A search whose own folder can't be read now fails instead of answering "no matches": `workspace_files::{search_files, search_contents}` and `fs_list::walk` return the root's `readdir` error (a sub-folder's is still swallowed), worded through the shared `claudius_server::fs_routes::search_failed` — 403 `Search failed: permission denied — can't read this folder (EACCES|EPERM)`, else 500 `Search failed: <node's scandir message>`. `listFs` therefore returns `io::Result`.
    - 49c5f28e + 554b9a9f: `session_search::first_hit` takes the first match that isn't on a raw JSONL line carrying the constant `"type":"api-request` (so `api-request-shape` / `-blob` / `-request` are skipped, jumping past the whole line as the TS moves `lastIndex`), and the snippet window is clamped to the matched line, so it never bleeds into the neighbouring entry.

  — done 2026-11-15 (background agent, with R08, resumed from the 2026-11-04 agent's partial work): new `claudius_core::process_group` (`KILL_GRACE` 1.5 s, `kill_process_group`). No new route.
    - SIGTERM goes to `-pid`, then SIGKILL 1.5 s later on a detached tokio task (a plain thread when off a runtime). If the SIGTERM finds no group (ESRCH), no SIGKILL is scheduled.
    - `bash_mode` (timeout + `dispose`) and `shell` (`execShellCommand`'s timeout) use it, so a `&` job that traps TERM no longer outlives the session or holds the pipes open. As in TS, nothing escalates on server shutdown.
    - Goldens re-recorded on the current TS: workspace-roots (TERM inside the grace, then the KILL of the shell and of a `&` job) and bash (+2 escalation scenarios: timeout, dispose).

"Platform detection moved onto a class" was a red herring: the class (`dD`) already existed in 0.3.299, and only the minified names moved (`Zq/Vq/ac` → `M9/iv/Bc`, `Yd` → `$p`, `vq` → `$4`, `Jn` → `xn`, `i0e` → `kje`, ...).
    - Ported: (1) `$p()` ignores `WSL_DISTRO_NAME` when the host's `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT` is exactly `remote_cowork` or `remote_cowork_trigger` (`d_`; case-sensitive, not trimmed). A Microsoft/WSL `/proc/version` still counts. `detect_wsl` (now pub) takes the entrypoint; new `COWORK_CLOUD_ENTRYPOINTS`. Claudius doesn't run as a cowork cloud session; a recorder control pair pins that the entrypoint is the gate.
    - Recorder: every patch site is found by shape and must match exactly once. Linux cases emulate the OS facts instead of patching the SDK's WSL answer: `process.platform` reads "linux" in `$p()`, in the loader and in the platform class; `/proc/version` is staged per case; the case's env is set; `xn` is redirected to a temp dir. So the SDK's own `$p()` result is recorded (`wsl`), and the replay's `detect_wsl` must reach it. All paths stay in temp dirs. The rewritten recorder is ASCII-only and lint-clean, which leaves four of R09's five non-ASCII recorders for I21 (`binary`, `fork`, `redact`, `sandbox-merge`).

    - Only for a reply to `initialize` (a success or an error reply); a non-initialize reply carrying the fields gets a debug log and nothing else. Truthy lists are walked in order: permissions first (entries whose `request.subtype` is the string `can_use_tool`, with `server_policy_ask_only` deleted), then dialogs (`request_user_dialog`, unchanged); other entries are skipped; falsy lists count as absent. Each entry goes through the live `handleControlRequest` path, started inline in list order before the `initialize` waiter is told.
    - A repeated id is skipped, whether it is already in flight or earlier in the same batch. `control_cancel_request` now also forgets the id (`handleControlCancelRequest`), so a cancelled request can be delivered again. Each in-flight registration has a generation, and `finally` only removes its own.
    - Malformed shapes make the SDK's reader throw, and Rust does the same. A non-array list (a string → "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'subtype')", else "e is not iterable"), a null entry, or a missing / null `request` ends the stream with the new `AgentError::TypeError(<V8 message>)`, or with "Claude Code returned an error result: …" after an error result with text. Every waiter fails with that error, in-flight callbacks are aborted, reading stops (lines after it in the same chunk too), stdin is ended, and SIGTERM follows only after the SDK's 2 s grace (`D9e`, new `CLOSE_GRACE`). Callbacks started before the throw still reply.
    - Differences: (1) a redelivered entry without a string `request_id` is dropped (`REDELIVERY_GAPS`: `odd_request_ids`; the same as the live-path gap); (2) after a throw, Rust answers the callbacks that settle on their first poll, where the SDK answers those that settle within microtasks (the same for every recorded case).

- [x] **R13** `QueryHandle::close()` kill timing (found by R12, 2026-11-15). `close()` sends SIGTERM as soon as it ends stdin and SIGKILL 2 s later (`KILL_GRACE`). SDK 0.3.312's `ProcessTransport.close()`, which its abort also runs, waits 2 s (`D9e`) before SIGTERM and 5 s more before SIGKILL; R12's reader-throw path waits the 2 s but also escalates to SIGKILL after 2 s. Read how session.ts ends a query (close vs abort) and match the SDK's timing where the TS host reaches it, without breaking the shutdown contract (PORT_PLAN *Shutdown contract*: `Session::end()` waits for each CLI, HTTP stops with a 3 s cap; `tests/shutdown.rs`). Pin it against the SDK with a fake CLI that exits on EOF, on SIGTERM, or ignores both. deps: R12
  — done 2026-11-16 (background agent): closing a CLI follows SDK 0.3.312's `ProcessTransport.close()` timer, in `claudius_agent::query`, and the shutdown contract holds.
    - SDK: `close()` ends stdin at once. If the CLI still runs, an unref'd `D9e` (2000 ms) timer checks again. If it exited, the timer only forwards the abort. Otherwise it sends `kill("SIGTERM")` and forwards the abort, and an unref'd 5000 ms timer sends `kill("SIGKILL")` while `exitCode === null`. Forwarding an aborted query's abort to the spawn's `signal` makes Node send a second SIGTERM in the same tick and emit `error`. That closes the reader: the stream ends with "Claude Code process aborted by user". On Windows there is no SIGTERM; the SIGKILL and then the forward come 7 s after the close.
    - TS paths: session.ts `end()` (`inputQueue.close()` + `abortController.abort()`) reaches only the transport's close, through the abort handler. Every `sessionManager.remove` calls it: the reaper, `dev-reap`, move-sessions, `restartIfDead`, `recoverInPlace`. The reader then runs until stdout closes ("Operation aborted") or until the forward. `Query.close()`, `return()` and the reader throw run `cleanup()`, which races `waitForExit()` with 2000 ms. `return()` and the host's stream (`readSdkMessages`' `finally`) await it. The one-shot callers (commit message, customization description, models probe) `return` out of `for await`. `interrupt` and stop-task are control requests and don't close. The TS server has no shutdown handler for sessions: Node's exit runs the SDK's `killAll()`, which SIGTERMs every CLI it still tracks and never SIGKILLs.
    - Rust: `QueryHandle::close()` (the abort) arms the same timer (`close_child`). After `CLOSE_GRACE` (2 s) it sends SIGTERM and forwards the abort, ending the stream with the new `AgentError::AbortedByUser`, then SIGKILL after `KILL_GRACE` (now 5 s). Before, it sent SIGTERM at once and SIGKILL 2 s later. New `QueryHandle::finish()` is `return()`: close, then wait for the exit, at most 2 s. `oneshot` and `models::probe_one` use it. The R12 reader-throw path uses the same timer (no SIGKILL after 2 s any more) and holds its error back until the exit or 2 s.
    - Shutdown: `claudius_agent::begin_exit()` runs in server main on SIGINT/SIGTERM, before the scheduler and sessions are ended. From then on every closed CLI, including one already in its grace, gets SIGTERM at once and SIGKILL 2 s later (`EXIT_KILL_GRACE`). `Session::end()` still waits for each CLI, shutdown is as fast as before, and the 3 s HTTP cap is unchanged.
    - Fake CLI: `on_eof` steps, `on_sigterm` (`"ignore"` / `"die"` / `{"exit", "after_ms"}`), and `$FAKE_CLAUDE_TIMES` (Unix ms of the EOF and of each trapped SIGTERM, one write per line, I22).
    - Differences:
      (1) Rust's `close()` is the abort, so the stream ends with the abort's error where the SDK's `Query.close()` ends it cleanly, at the same time. No lib/server caller uses `Query.close()` (`CLOSE_STREAM_ERROR_GAP`).
      (2) The forward sends one SIGTERM; the SDK sends two in one tick.
      (3) During a shutdown, Rust SIGKILLs a CLI that ignores SIGTERM 2 s later and waits for it; a TS host leaves it orphaned. A query nobody closed (an in-flight one-shot) gets no SIGTERM at exit; `kill_on_drop` SIGKILLs it when the runtime drops.
      (4) `Session::end()` waits for the CLI (at most 5 s); TS `end()` returns at once. Outside a shutdown, ending a session whose CLI ignores EOF (mid-turn) now takes the 2 s grace instead of being immediate. A CLI that also ignores SIGTERM is SIGKILLed by the waiter task 7 s after the close, after `end()` gave up at 5 s.
      (5) After a reader throw at a CLI that also ignores SIGTERM, `oneshot` and the probe can wait up to 2 s longer than TS, because TS doesn't call `return()` on a throw.
      (6) A scheduler run stopped by the shutdown now has the note "Claude Code process aborted by user" (was "Operation aborted"): its CLI is mid-turn, so the immediate SIGTERM forwards the abort.
      (7) Windows is type-checked only.
    - Tests: `close_timing_golden.rs` replays `close_timing_golden.json`, recorded by `rust/scripts/close-timing-golden.mjs` on SDK 0.3.312. It covers 4 paths (`Query.close()`, the abort, `return()`, the R12 reader throw) × 4 fakes (exits at EOF, dies by SIGTERM, ignores both, exits 3.5 s after SIGTERM), 3 rounds per recording, in coarse buckets. Three recordings were byte-identical, the last at load ~300. 16/16 cases match. Also `fixture_pins_the_two_timers`, `oneshot_waits_for_the_exit_like_return`, and `claudius-server/tests/models.rs` `probe_waits_for_its_cli_like_return`. `tests/shutdown.rs` `sigterm_skips_the_close_grace_and_waits_for_a_cli_that_ignores_it` checks SIGTERM within 1.5 s and a shutdown that waits at least 1.8 s and takes under 4.5 s. The `scheduler_boot` note expectation is updated. The recorder is ASCII-only and lint-clean. The resumed agent found the previous agent's work complete, re-recorded the fixture byte-identically, moved the fake's `$FAKE_CLAUDE_TIMES` writes onto `append_line` (EOF and SIGTERM raced and tore lines), and made the Windows forward come with the kill.
    - Mutation 28/28 (16 in query.rs / error.rs, 5 in the fake, 6 on the exit path, 1 in models.rs), each killed for the intended reason. Not mutated, equivalent: the `biased;` tie-breaks, and the second `forward.cancel()` after the kill, which is redundant on unix (it is the Windows path). ## 5P. Performance (found 2026-11-23, perf report)

The 23 Nov benchmark ran the production Next build against the release `claudius` on the same data: this project's 35 real transcripts (245 MB, the largest 107 MB) and a clone of the repo. The report is `rust/target/par/status/perf-2026-11-23.html` and the scripts are in `rust/target/par/tools/perf/`, both gitignored; F01 moves the scripts into the repo. Rust was faster on 8 of the 11 requests timed, started in 49 ms against 0.99 s, served 5–7× the requests per second, and kept tiny requests at 0.2 ms (p95) while big transcripts loaded, where TS's went to 39 ms. These tasks are where it lost: memory after heavy use, very large transcripts, and the warm cost overview. Each one names its target on the F01 world.

- [ ] **F01** Perf harness in the repo. Move `rust/target/par/tools/perf/` to `rust/scripts/perf/`: `setup-world.sh`, `run-backend.sh`, `run-all.sh`, `run-combo.sh`, `bench.mjs`, `phase-probe.mjs`, `hol-probe.mjs`, and the report builder (`build-report.mjs`, `report-*.html`/`.css`). `setup-world.sh` copies the user's real transcripts today. Give it a synthetic generator of realistic transcript lines (a 1.7 MB median file, one of 107 MB, 35 in all), and keep `--transcripts <dir>` for real data. Record the 23 Nov figures in `rust/parity/perf.md` as the baseline (the `perf-*.jsonl` files beside the scripts), and re-run after each F task. deps: none
- [ ] **F02** Session search memory (`GET /api/sessions/search`, `claudius_core::session_search::search_with`).
  - **Problem:** each worker reads a whole transcript (`Limits::read_utf8`) and copies it to UTF-16 (`encode_utf16().collect()`), `CONCURRENCY` workers at once. That's about 320 MB for the 107 MB file, and macOS keeps the freed pages. Three searches left 1.5 GB held (macOS footprint agrees with RSS), against 565 MB for TS.
  - **Fix:** decode and scan in bounded chunks that overlap by the query's length in UTF-16 units, and cap the bytes in flight across workers.
  - **Same results:** the same `canonicalize` folding, the first hit per file, UTF-16 snippet offsets, and the `TooLarge` / max-string refusals at node's sizes.
  - **Tests:** the existing search tests unchanged, plus a hit straddling a chunk boundary, a surrogate pair split by a boundary, and a hit that only matches after folding.
  - **Target:** the phase probe's `search ×3` adds ≤ 100 MB of held memory over the step before; median ≤ 206 ms (today's).
  - deps: F01
- [ ] **F03** Transcript loads (`GET /api/sessions/transcript/[id]` and `GET /api/sessions/[id]/transcript`; `sessions_store::messages` → `transcript::read_file` + `transcript::session_messages`).
  - **Problem:** every call reads the whole file and builds a `serde_json::Value` per line, even to return the last 50 messages. TS does the same (`getSessionMessages`), but faster. On the 107 MB transcript:
    - full load: 635 ms against TS's 509 ms;
    - last 50 messages: 636 ms against 468 ms;
    - memory: one load peaks around 800 MB, four at once at 3.1 GB.
  - **Fix:**
    - (a) profile the full load (building the `Value`s, the chain walk, `js::stringify`) and get it under TS's time;
    - (b) cache each file's chain, or the byte offsets of its message lines, keyed by path + size + mtime, so a page reads only its 50 messages;
    - (c) drop lines that aren't on the chain while parsing, or parse into borrowed structures.
  - **Same output byte for byte:** the existing transcript fixtures, plus a page-vs-full comparison on a large generated file.
  - **Targets:** full 107 MB ≤ 500 ms, a warm page of 50 ≤ 50 ms, one load's peak ≤ 300 MB above idle.
  - deps: F01
- [ ] **F04** Give freed memory back.
  - **Problem:** the binary uses the system allocator, and on macOS freed memory stays in the process. After the phase probe it held 2.4 GB; TS's garbage collector got back down to 0.9 GB at one point.
  - **Options:**
    - mimalloc;
    - jemalloc (`tikv-jemallocator` with `background_thread` and a short `dirty_decay_ms`) as the `#[global_allocator]` of `claudius-server`'s binary;
    - a cheaper one: `malloc_zone_pressure_relief` after big requests.
  - **Measure:** held memory 30 s after the phase probe, request medians (no regression over 5%) and binary size.
  - **Build check:** build both arm64 and x64, since N09 ships a universal binary. A C dependency must cross-compile; re2's Electron dead end in CLAUDE.md is the cautionary tale, though this binary isn't loaded by Electron.
  - deps: F01
- [ ] **F05** Cost overview, warm (`GET /api/cost/overview`; `claudius_core::cost_aggregate` and the overview).
  - **Problem:** both backends keep the per-project `.claudius-cost-cache.json`. Once warm, TS answers in 17 ms and Rust in 57 ms; cold, Rust is faster (0.18 s against 1.2 s).
  - **Fix:** profile the warm path (re-reading and re-parsing the cache file, stat'ing every transcript, re-pricing rows). Keep in memory what TS keeps: `getCostOverview`'s in-flight join and the parsed cache, invalidated the same way.
  - **Target:** warm ≤ 17 ms, cold no slower.
  - deps: F01
- [ ] **F06** Re-check the Rust + Next (pages) shape, the one the first release would ship.
  - **Problem:** in its one run, heavy requests were 2–9× slower than with Rust alone:
    - search: 384 ms against 206 ms;
    - git status: 236 against 54;
    - file name search: 187 against 21;
    - cost overview: 195 against 57.

    The two processes held 1.34 GB after the bench, and Next grew although it served no API. On its own, Next serving only pages idled at 130–160 MB and scanned nothing.
  - **Fix:** re-run 3× (`run-combo.sh`), recording each process's memory separately. If the slowdown holds, find what Next still does with `CLAUDIUS_RUST_SCHEDULER=1` (instrumentation, watchers, seeding scans, the session manager), and what `claudius --next` does that `--no-next` doesn't. Then stop the duplicate work.
  - deps: F01
- [ ] **F07** Memory budget: the acceptance for F02–F04. The user asked that Rust use less memory than the TS backend did. On the F01 world's phase probe, Rust must stay below TS at every step:
  - held after the full sequence: ≤ 50% of TS's (TS 1.52 GB, so ≤ 760 MB);
  - peak: ≤ TS's peak (1.56 GB), including four 107 MB loads at once;
  - idle: ≤ 20 MB.

  Add the phase probe with these thresholds to `rust/scripts/perf/` as a check that fails loudly, and run it before T01. deps: F02, F03, F04

Root: `activeId ?? workspaces[0]` → `/<id>`, else `/welcome`. Stubs: `activeId` → `/<id><path>`, else `/`. Welcome: any workspace → `/`.

        - `router.replace` matches the old reach: a `redirect()` under the root `<Suspense>` arrived as a client-side replace.
        - Nothing renders until the decision, so the splash never flashes. A failed read shows a one-line `workspace-redirect-error`.
        - `/`, `/welcome` and the stubs are now static in `bun run build`.
      - **Row 3** `[workspaceId]/layout.tsx`: a server component that reads nothing (N04 adds gSP there), rendering `<WorkspaceIdGuard>`.
        - An id in the shared `useWorkspaces` list renders at once, with no extra request: the store's one fetch is the one the pages make anyway.
        - An id missing from the list is checked on its own, with `GET /api/workspaces/<id>` (the old `getWorkspace`, R4's check). Only a 404 → `router.replace("/")`; any other failure, or a failed list read, renders the pages.
        - Nothing renders until the check answers, so a stale id mounts no chat and creates no session.
        - Once an id has been seen, its pages stay mounted whatever the list does later. `WorkspaceSwitcher`'s delete of the open workspace does its own `location.href = "/"`.
        - The workspace pages' SSR HTML no longer has their content (the guard is `null` on the server); they render after hydration plus the store's fetch.
      - **Two designs dropped on the way:**
        - Re-reading the list and then trusting `/`: behind Rust, a list that disagrees with the store loops forever. That happened with `customizations-drawer`'s stubbed list: R1 sends `/` to the real workspace, the guard sends it back to `/`, and so on.
        - A latch to break that loop: behind Rust the bounce keeps the URL and the guard instance, so nothing reliable marks the return trip. The two versions tried either missed it (the page stayed blank) or could, in principle, mount a stale id.
        - The per-id check has neither problem: the server answers for the id itself.
      - `lib/server/redirect-to-workspace.ts` is deleted. The 2026-11-22 note kept it, but nothing imports it now. `proxy.ts` stays.
      - The "bare redirect stub" recipe in `scripts/implement-workflow.mjs` / `-2.mjs` now says `<BareRouteRedirect />`, plus the name in `SCOPED_ROUTES` (`proxy.ts`) and `SCOPED` (`pages.rs`).
      - The `pages.rs` docs are updated: the module doc no longer says "N03 deletes `proxy.ts`", and two comments no longer name `redirectToWorkspaceRoute`. `rust/parity/server-components.md` § 3 marks rows 2, 3, 4 and 6 done.
      - Tests:
        - New `tests/e2e/workspace-redirects.spec.ts` (5 tests, both backends unchanged): `/?probe=1` keeps its query; bare `/git` and catch-all `/dev/chat-empty` with no cookie go under the active workspace; `/welcome` with a workspace goes to it, and the splash never appears (a MutationObserver); `/wks_000000000000` lands on the active workspace with no `POST /api/sessions` made from the stale URL.
        - Mutants, both caught by the stale-id test: the guard as a pass-through, and the guard letting a 404 through.
        - `customizations-drawer.spec.ts`: its fixture workspace id `wks_fixture_project` → `wks_f1f1f1f1f1f1`. With Next alone, `/` now resolves in the browser from the spec's stubbed list, and use-session only writes `?session=` on a `/wks_<hex>` path, so 'Manage all' timed out waiting for it.
        - Targeted (the drawer, the new spec, the three other specs that stub `/api/workspaces`, `--repeat-each=2`): Rust 36/36, TS 36/36.
        - N03a's 20 spec files plus the new spec, `app-functionality`, `2.1.216-sandbox-filesystem-disabled` and `2.1.248-restricted-mode`, on the dropped latch design: Rust 63/63, TS 62/63 (the drawer, before its fixture fix). The full parity runs below are on the final code.
        - Lint is clean on all 30 touched TS / TSX / MJS files. Whole-project `tsc` is clean. `bun run build` passes. No unit test covers these files.
        - **Full parity e2e on the final code, 473 tests (230 spec files): Rust 465 passed, 1 failed, 7 skipped (11.2 min); TS 465 / 1 / 7 (17.0 min).** The one failure on each is `cc-parity-2.1.222-diff-raw-content` (I25, `pre-existing`). `rust/parity/e2e-status.md` was regenerated with this TS run as the baseline.
- [ ] **N04** Static build of the UI (`output: "export"` or an equivalent pipeline). Handle dynamic segments (`[workspaceId]`, `sessions/[[...rest]]`, `customize/[id]`, `dev/[[...rest]]`) with an SPA fallback in axum.
  Carried over from N01's audit and N02:
  - Export mode rejects a dynamic page without `generateStaticParams`, and that export can't come from a `"use client"` file, so each segment needs a server layout with one placeholder param (`__ws__`, `__sid__`, `__cust__`) and `dynamicParams = false`. axum then maps real ids onto the placeholder's files.
  - The five `useParams()` / `use(params)` readers must parse `usePathname()` first (N03), or they read the placeholder: `[workspaceId]/page.tsx`, `[workspaceId]/sessions/page.tsx`, `[workspaceId]/sessions/[id]/page.tsx`, `customize/[id]/page.tsx`, `components/chat/SystemPill.tsx`.
  - `claudius_server::pages` (N02) only answers document paths today: its matcher drops anything with a `.` in it, so the router payload URLs (`<p>.txt`, `/index.txt`, `<p>/__next.<segments>.txt`) fall through. In export mode they must go through the same rules, with the suffix stripped and re-appended.

## 7. Native Rust UI (replaces React + Electron)

**2026-11-26, the user: GUI first.** This section now goes before 5R/5P/6, and tasks may start ahead of their nominal deps when a fixed contract lets them run in parallel: U02 against U01k's stream contract, and U04a against a fixture-backed `ShellData` trait. The pass started 2026-11-26 runs U01p+U01k, U02 and U04a in parallel clones (`rust/target/par/{U01k,U02,U04a}`, briefs in `rust/target/par/briefs/`).

Full numbers, versions, licenses, the 13 problems hit, how to rerun, and the pending manual checks are in `docs/adr-ui-toolkit.md` ("Spike findings"). Everything below was measured on an M5 Max at 120 Hz under heavy load (1-min load 120–930 on 18 cores), as the median of 3 runs. "CPU" is the main thread's CPU time per frame.
      - Scroll: on 10k synthetic rows, 119.3 fps, frame interval p99 17.0 ms, CPU p99 10.0 ms (2.7% of frames over the 8.33 ms budget).
      - Real 10k events (7,800 rows) rendered in full: 99 fps, CPU p95 21.5 ms, worst frame 281 ms. The cost is real work, not load: 12–30 KB rows (pasted prompts, huge tool inputs) are laid out in one frame. With rows over 4 KB folded: 115.6 fps, CPU p95 6.3 ms. Folded and pre-parsed: 117.4 fps, CPU p95 5.2 ms; at 4,800 px/s, CPU p99 8.1 ms. Requirement for U05/U06: fold big rows.
      - The real source: `--resume` replays only what follows the last compaction (≤ 610 events in the 4 transcripts probed), so `--raw-replay` feeds the first 10,000 entries of a real 13,645-entry transcript through the same session. It reads the file in place: no copy, and the screenshots use synthetic content only.
      - Streaming: 119.9 fps, interval p99 10.0 ms; delta-to-pixel latency p50 / p95 / p99 7.1 / 11.0 / 12.4 ms. `push_str` deltas; a full re-parse per delta raised CPU p95 from 3.7 to 9.9 ms.
      - Memory: peak RSS 394 MB (synthetic) / 353 MB (real). Pre-parsing all 10k rows gave 667 MB, so keep parsed state near the viewport only.
      - Cold start (under load): exec→main 127–203 ms, main→first frame 872 ms, main→whole 10k session painted 3.6 s.
      - Rich elements pass: markdown, tables, code, diff and images. gpui-component's `Diff` rendered at zero height until wrapped in `flex_shrink_0`, and a fenced ```` ```diff ```` gets no red/green from syntect.
      - Math: RaTeX 0.1.16 (MIT, KaTeX fonts OFL-1.1) takes 0.2–0.8 ms per formula. Against KaTeX 0.19 on 8 formulas, widths are equal on 5 and within 3.4% on the rest; `\left(` delimiters are 18% too tall.
      - Selection and copy: a drag across rows 7–11 selected 728 characters, ⌘C matched `pbpaste`. A drag that starts in a table cell stays in that cell (gpui-component's choice).
      - HTML pane (`gpui-webview` / lb-wry): scripts run with origin `null`, and storage, `fetch` and `window.open` are blocked; top-level navigation is denied.
      - IME: the composer's input handler took preedit にほんご, conversion to 日本語, and commit, with nothing sent. The real macOS input method is a manual check (U00b).
      - Binary: 40.2 MB as built, 31.0 MB stripped, 13.1 MB gzipped. 557 crates, with no GPL, AGPL or LGPL. MPL-2.0 only for option-ext (linked unmodified) and cbindgen (build only). gpui-kit / gpui-component 0.10.0 and gpui-pre `=0.3.12` are Apache-2.0. No Zed GPL code was copied.
      - Hand-offs:
        - U02: `replay_done` is sent at subscribe time, so an in-process subscriber gets no marker when the disk replay ends; U02 defines one.
        - U03: theme colors come from `cx.theme()`, and `Theme::change` switches light and dark.
        - U05: RaTeX output is rasterized at scale 1, so draw it natively or rasterize at the window scale, and re-render on theme change. gpui-component has no public way to give a pre-parsed `TextViewState` its plugins (an upstream PR candidate). `MessageScroller` hides its `ListState`, so the spike uses GPUI's `list()`.
        - U08: the React AskUserQuestion preview never runs scripts (`dangerouslySetInnerHTML`), while other iframes allow same-origin; pick one. Without the optional `gpui-fast` backend, the native WebView covers popovers and menus.
        - U21: GPUI caps inactive or occluded windows at 30 fps, or stops drawing them.
        - Expect API breaks on every gpui-component bump (0.7 → 0.10 in six weeks).
      - Side effects, cleaned up: the spike leaked its temp sandbox dir on every run (41 removed; it now removes it on quit). Its selftest overwrote the system clipboard on its early runs; it now saves and restores it.

A task's model is stamped once (empty counts as none). `paused` is non-terminal. A real task beats a provisional row, and the dropped row's `startedAt` carries over.
      - JS own-key order: index keys first.
      - Thinking estimate: it goes on the latest open row with the matching stamp, else the latest open row (an empty uuid counts as none).
      - Numbers and fields: `endedAt: 0` is kept; NaN, ±Infinity, -0, fractional attempts and clocks; tool rows keep `parentToolUseId: null`.
      - Prompt queue: a re-emitted prompt keeps its place, and settled ids never come back.
      - System entries coalesce from count 1.
      - Boot guard: storage that throws `SecurityError` on any op; foreign, malformed, expired or other-load markers; the forced flag is consumed once.
      - `effort: null` vs an absent `effort`.
      - Workflow `meta`: streaming (truncated) input, escapes, all three quote kinds, JS whitespace, surrogates.

    - Behaviours found in the TS (all mirrored):
      - `holder_changed` without a `holderId`, or with a non-string one, makes the tab read-only (`undefined !== null`).
      - Event fields are stored unchecked: tips `"nope"`, cwd `42`, mode `undefined`. An `error` with no message pushes `undefined`.
      - The MCP notices throw V8 TypeErrors on an odd `servers` (`null` / `undefined` `.length`, `.join` not a function, an object element). The hook catches them and logs them as bad SSE payloads. An empty list still shows "MCP servers need auth:  · open /mcp to connect".
      - A `null` message throws reading `'type'`; primitives and arrays are ignored.
      - `session_recap_error` acts only while a recap is loading. A reason other than `"failed"` goes back to idle.
      - `requestRecap` keeps the previous text, `at` and `errorReason` while loading. A 500 or a network failure goes back to idle; a 202 with an empty body stays loading.
      - `goal_changed` uses truthiness: `""` clears the goal, while `5` or an object becomes the text.
      - `turn_status` with a missing or odd status settles the turn without reconciling. `backgroundTasks` is stored as sent, and `null` as 0.
      - The poll fires every 30 s but fetches only while a prompt is pending or a listed session is running, and never while the page is hidden.
      - `refreshSessions`:
        - keeps `sessions` on any throw, by which point `accountsConfigured` has already been set;
        - doesn't throw on a lone row's uncomparable `lastModified`;
        - keeps live sessions past the top 20;
        - accepts a BOM before the JSON.
      - The pending-prompts merge skips duplicate `requestId`s, ignores lists that aren't arrays and replies that aren't ok, and reads a body that isn't JSON as `{}`.

    — done 2026-11-22 (background agent): the `sdk` event's `user` and `tool_progress` messages, `system` / `thinking_tokens`, and the assistant split's Edit / MultiEdit / Write, background Bash and KillBash side effects, in a new module `user_events.rs`. `sdk.rs` gets the dispatch arms and a one-line hook in the tool_use loop; `state.rs` types `recentEdits` as `RecentEdit`.
      - Events:
        - `user` with a `tool_result` (checked first, so a subagent's or a synthetic record's result lands too):
          - the result goes on every tool_use with its id, in the transcript and in every subagent trail;
          - the tool's progress frame is dropped, and its rail row and recent edit are marked done (`endedAt`, `isError`, a sticky `notRun`);
          - a Task's status follows its result (`reconcileTasksOnToolResult`, skipping a backgrounded launch), and a Workflow ack seeds a provisional task (`upsertProvisionalTask` + `parseWorkflowMeta`);
          - a tracked background shell takes its SDK-side id from the ack text, and a Bash result's `tool_use_result` backgrounds a foreground shell (`applyBashAutoBackground`).
        - Any other `user`, on a subagent: its prompt joins its trail.
        - On the main thread:
          - the compaction summary becomes or enriches a divider (`mergeCompactBoundary`);
          - the internal records are dropped (`isMeta` / `isCompactSummary` / `isVisibleInTranscriptOnly`, the local-command caveat, the image-resize note, `<task-notification>`);
          - a replayed SDK slash command and the CLI's `<command-name>` / stdout / stderr wrappers become info pills; the prose after a wrapper is its own `:trailing` bubble, and "Set model to X" only sets `model`;
          - leading `<system-reminder>`s become reminder pills, and the rest is a user bubble with its images and a peer sender.
        - `tool_progress` goes into `toolProgress`; `thinking_tokens` goes through `applyThinkingTokensEstimate`. `thinking_tokens` is a guarded `Some("system") if is_thinking_tokens(msg)` arm; keep it above U01h's `system` dispatch (no earlier subtype intercepts it in the TS, and the catch-all suppresses it).

      - Behaviours found in the TS (all mirrored):
        - Results:
          - Only a message's first `tool_result` counts. `extractToolResult` reads `.type` on every block first, so a `null` block throws (a bad SSE payload) before anything changes, even on a plain prompt.
          - A repeat result overwrites the content and re-stamps `endedAt`. `isError` is overwritten (a result without `is_error` clears it), while `notRun` sticks once set. `staged` / `detached` need a strict `true` on an object `tool_use_result`.
        - Tool lookup: the tool is looked up in the sorted transcript as last rendered (`messagesRef.current`). For a shared id the chronologically earlier tool_use decides. Trails aren't searched, so a subagent's Workflow seeds no task.
        - Workflow tasks:
          - None on `is_error: true`.
          - The description is `meta.description` (`""` kept), else `Workflow <name>` for a non-empty name, else "Workflow running in background".
          - Only an `export const meta` is read, and a second ack changes nothing.
        - Shell ids:
          - The ack text is matched with `/bash[_-]?(?:id)?[: ]+([\w-]+)/i` (ASCII). The CLI's "Command running in background with ID: bash_1" doesn't match (that id comes from `backgroundTaskId`). "BASH ID: Abc_9" gives `ID`, and a `""` id counts as none.
          - Any Bash result (not only `run_in_background`) can be auto-backgrounded, and another tool's `backgroundTaskId` is ignored. The start is `at ?? now`.
        - Subagent prompts: a string, or the joined text parts (`type === "text"` exactly, truthy text, numbers stringified), once per uuid per trail. `parent_tool_use_id: ""` is the main thread. A subagent record flagged `isSynthetic` still joins its trail (the parent is checked first). An empty text uses no uuid.
        - Flags and the compaction divider: `isSynthetic` and the envelope flags need a strict `true`, and a falsy `message` shows nothing, even with `isCompactSummary`. The summary divider merges with one at the same anchor, and its text is trimmed.
        - Slash and CLI pills:
          - Slash pills are only for the SDK-handled names, once per uuid ("Ran /compact focus", "Ran /review").
          - CLI labels are "Ran /x args", "CLI: text" / "CLI ran" and "CLI error: text" / "CLI error". Trailing prose that opens with `<` isn't a bubble, and the trailing bubble has no `createdAt`.
          - "Set model to" is ASCII-case-insensitive with JS `\s`. It takes the token before its first `[`, does nothing when that is empty, and never makes a pill.
        - Reminders: one pill per leading `<system-reminder>` (`<uuid>-reminder-<i>`, after the last assistant message), even when the bubble is a duplicate or empty. An image inside a reminder moves its `[Image #n]` into the pill, which leaves an image-only bubble with `blocks: []`.
        - Bubbles: text or images, once per uuid (a re-send with other text keeps the first). A peer's non-empty `body` replaces the text, and shows even when the text is empty. `createdAt` is set only for a numeric `at`, and whitespace-only text is a bubble. A missing or `null` uuid takes a random one even when nothing shows.
        - `tool_progress`: one frame per id, replaced in place, with index-like ids (`"7"`, `"123"`) first. The result drops it, and `turn_status: idle` / `result` clear it.
        - `thinking_tokens`:
          - Only a number counts: `"400"`, `null` and a missing count are ignored; `-0` and `0.5` are kept.
          - It goes on the open row stamped with `user_message_uuid`, else on the oldest open row (`findLastIndex` over the newest-first rail), which takes the stamp. `""` stamps nothing.
          - Closed rows take none.
        - Recent edits: Edit / MultiEdit / Write (case-sensitive) with a non-empty string `file_path`, once per id, newest first, 20 kept, started at now (not `at`). A subagent's edit isn't tracked.
        - Background Bash and KillBash: a shell needs `run_in_background === true` and a string `command`, once per id (a replay keeps the first start; `at: 0` is kept). KillBash reads `shell_id ?? bash_id` (a `null` falls back, a number stops) and only matches a tool_use id.
      - Differences, deliberate (crate convention), none in the replayed steps (`user_events::tests::odd_wire_fields_read_as_missing`):
        - a non-string `uuid` takes a random one (the TS keeps the number);
        - a non-string `parent_tool_use_id` is the main thread (the TS keys a trail by it);
        - a non-number `at` starts a shell at now (the TS stores it as sent);
        - a non-string `user_message_uuid` stamps nothing.
        - U01a's `tool_progress` conventions show on a frame without `parent_tool_use_id` or with a partial `subagent_retry`; the recorder sends neither.

    - Behaviours found in the TS (all mirrored):
      - Re-emits and the merge:
        - A request seen leaving (answered here or `prompt_settled`) is ignored when re-emitted, and skipped by the `/pending-prompts` merge.
        - A duplicate `requestId` replaces the queued prompt in place.
        - Prompts queue before `ready` and during the replay.
      - `prompt_settled`:
        - For an unknown id it still marks the id gone.
        - Any `kind` other than `"permission"` / `"ask"` (`"plan"`, `"PERMISSION"`, `null`, `5`, missing) goes to the elicitation queue, so a plan is never settled by it.
        - The same id in two queues: settling one kind marks it gone for both, so later re-emits of either kind are ignored, but the other queue keeps its copy.
      - The answers:
        - Every answer drops the prompt and marks it gone before the POST, also for an id not queued or already settled.
        - Nothing reads any reply: a 500, a 404, a non-JSON body or a network failure all leave the change in place.
        - `setPermissionMode` never rolls back; only `mode_changed` corrects it.
        - Only `resolvePermission`'s network failure surfaces, as an unhandled rejection ("TypeError: Failed to fetch"); the other four swallow it.
        - Every action needs a truthy bound session (`""` counts as none).
      - Plans:
        - `resolvePlan` with no plan pending does nothing. A plan without `requestId` POSTs `{"decision":…}`; a non-string one is sent as is (`5`).
        - `raw` is kept when truthy (`[]`, `{}`, `"x"`) and dropped for `0`, `""` or `null`; `toolUseId: null` is kept.
    - Differences (deliberate): as in U01m's `Prompt`, a non-string `requestId` keys the queues and the gone set as `""`, where the TS uses `===` and a `Set`.
      - So `5` and `{}` collide here.
      - A `prompt_settled` without an id also drops a queued `null`- or number-id prompt.
      - The server always sends a UUID string.
      - Not in the fixture; `prompts::tests::odd_ids_key_as_empty_strings` pins it. The replayed odd ids (missing, `7`, `null`) agree.

          - TaskList's result rebuilds the rail: "No tasks found" leaves it, a blank text clears it, and an error is ignored.
          - A subagent's tool calls don't touch the rail, but a subagent's tool result does re-key a TaskCreate.

          - Its result moves it under `/job\s+([a-z0-9]+)\s*\(([^)]+)\)/i` group 1 (ASCII `/i`, JS `\s`: "JOB\tq9 (Every\nday)", "job k7\t(Hourly)"), with the human schedule, durable unless `/session[- ]only/i` ("session only" counts), and `nextFireAt` from `tool_use_result`. A failed result drops it, and a promoted id can collide and replace in place.
          - CronDelete marks a loop cancelled.

          - A stop applies once per tool_use and only to wake-ups armed at or before its `at`. A wake-up's result is never promoted.

    — done 2026-11-23 (background agent): `moveToActiveAccount` and `effortChangeWouldWait`, in a new module `account.rs`, plus the crate's promise-outcome channel.
      - Design (the merge loop's call, U01h precedent, generalised): every action whose promise has a value ends with one `Effect::ActionResolved { action: Action, outcome: Value }`. `outcome` is the promise's value as the TS builds it, in snapshot JSON. It arrives in the action's own return for an early `return`, else in the `fetch_settled` of the reply the TS awaited last. `Action` names the call, with the argument that tells two calls apart where the action has one (`JumpToUuid { target }`, `UpdateTodoItem { item_id }`, `EditQueued { qid }`). Void actions emit nothing; uncaught rejections stay `Effect::UnhandledRejection`. `effect.rs` documents the convention.
      - Folded in, all test-covered: U01h's `Effect::JumpResolved` (5 unit tests in `system_events.rs`) and U01g's `Effect::TodoItemUpdated` (its unit test and the `task-` scenarios' `resolved` steps). The golden harness now checks each outcome and its action's name against the recorder's `resolved` value.
      - `move_to_active_account`: `POST /api/sessions/{id}/account` with no headers and no body; no state changes.
      - `effort_change_would_wait(level)`: a pure read through `next_effort_wait`, which `set_effort` now shares (the TS's `nextEffortWait`).
      - Behaviours found in the TS (all mirrored):
        - `moveToActiveAccount`: a 2xx is `{ok: true}` and its body is never read (a non-JSON 200 or an empty 201 is fine). A non-2xx gives `body.error ?? "HTTP <status>"` off `res.json().catch(() => ({}))`; a numeric or object error is kept as sent (`5`, `{code}`); `error: null`, a non-JSON body or a JSON string body gives the HTTP text; a `null` body throws inside the `try`, so the value is `{ok: false, error: "Cannot read properties of null (reading 'error')"}`. A network failure gives `err.message` ("Failed to fetch"). Nothing changes locally: `account` updates only from the restarted query's fresh `ready`. `if (!id)` also rejects `""`.
        - `effortChangeWouldWait`: needs no session. Idle it is false. Mid-turn it compares against the applied level, else the shown pick; after a mid-turn pick, against the level in force from the window's first pick. False after `effort_applied: null` and after the turn ends. It changes no state.
      - Differences: none in the replayed steps. Outcomes are JSON values (the TS's own shapes), not typed results.
      - Not replayed (unit-tested): `moveToActiveAccount` without a bound session (`None` / `""`), because the recorder's boot always binds `sess-1`.

    — done 2026-11-23 (background agent): the queue mirror and the send / queue actions, in a new module `queue.rs`, rebased onto U01g.
      - Event: `queue:updated`. The snapshot replaces `queue` wholesale with `{id: uuid, text}` plus each truthy flag as `true`; `sdkQueuedTurns ?? 0` is stored as sent.
      - Actions, each an optimistic change plus a fetch: promise-valued (they end with an `Effect::ActionResolved`): `send` (`SendImage`, `SendOpts`), `enqueue`, `edit_queued`, `rename_title`, `set_goal` / `clear_goal`, `submit_feedback` (`FeedbackInput`); void: `cancel_queued`, `reorder_queued`, `send_queued_now`, `send_all_queued_now`, `interrupt`, `take_over`.
      - Checked against the TS: U01c's `requestRecap` / `dismissRecap`, one gap fixed (`if (!id)` also rejects `""`, which the port had let through; unit-tested). U01i's `dismissFeedback` matches.
      - Behaviours found in the TS (all mirrored):
        - `queue:updated`: applies before `ready` too. A non-object row maps to `{}`, a missing `uuid` / `text` is left out, a `null` text is kept. Falsy flags (`0`, `""`, `null`) are dropped; `"yes"` is kept as `true`. `sdkQueuedTurns`: `null` gives 0, a string is kept. A missing or `null` queue, a non-array (a string or an object), or a `null` row throws (a V8 TypeError, logged as a bad payload) before anything changes, `sdkQueuedTurns` included.
        - `send`: blank after JS `trim` (NBSP and BOM count as blank) with no images: `{ok: true}` and no POST. No session: `{ok: false, error: "no session"}`. Before the POST it clears the recap banner, the prompt suggestions, `errors` and `processExited`; they stay cleared when the POST fails. The optimistic bubble holds the untrimmed text (`pending`, `createdAt`); images are normalised (a missing id is minted before the message uuid, a non-number ordinal becomes its 1-based position). A slash command gets no bubble. The suggestion / goal badges are added before the POST and never rolled back. The body text is untrimmed; `inlinePastes` only when not empty. A network failure raises "send failed: couldn't reach Claudius" (not with `reportsFailureInline`), rolls the bubble back and resolves `{ok: false, error: "network"}`. A non-2xx raises `send failed: <status>`, rolls back and resolves `http <status>`; on a 503 a truthy `error` replaces the status, stringified (`5`, `["a","b"]` as "a,b"); `""`, `null` or non-JSON keep "503". A 2xx with a truthy `queued` (`1` counts, `""` doesn't) drops the bubble; a non-JSON or `null` body keeps it; a BOM body is parsed.
        - `enqueue`: the text is trimmed; images only when not empty; `forceQueue: true`; no bubble. A failure resolves `http <status>` or `network`.
        - cancel / reorder / send now / send all: the reply is ignored and a failure swallowed; `qid` goes into the URL raw (`q 9`); `reorderQueued`: `-1` is "up", anything else (`0` included) is "down".
        - `editQueued`: resolves `{text, images?}` only for a 2xx JSON object whose `text` is a string (`""` counts); `images` kept as sent when `.length` is truthy (a non-empty array or string, or an object with a truthy `length`; a number is dropped); `null` for a non-2xx (even with a text), a network failure, non-JSON, a `null` or string body, or a non-string text.
        - `interrupt`: any reply (500 and 404 included) settles the turn. Only the string items of `stillQueued` count; the "Stop: N queued message(s) will still run" pill is anchored after the last assistant message, and a 500 carrying `stillQueued` still raises it. A non-array, non-JSON or `null` body raises no pill. A network failure is an unhandled rejection, and the turn stays pending.
        - `takeOver`: a PATCH with this tab's id; only `holder_changed` changes `readOnly`; a network failure is an unhandled rejection.
        - `renameTitle`: the trimmed title shows at once; blank gives "title required", no session "no active session". A failure reverts to the title the call started from, not one an SSE echo set meanwhile; the error is `body.error ?? "HTTP <status>"`, V8's message for a `null` body, or "Failed to fetch". A success runs `refreshSessions`.
        - `setGoal` / `clearGoal`: optimistic `{text: trimmed, achieved: false, summary: null, setAt: now, achievedAt: null}`, or `null` for a clear; a failure reverts to the goal the call started from. A blank `setGoal` POSTs `{goal: null}`, the same as `clearGoal()`. Errors as `renameTitle`'s.
        - `submitFeedback`: body `{sessionId, rating?, comment, attachTranscript?}`. A 2xx gives `{ok: j.ok ?? true, stored: j.stored ?? true, forwarded: j.forwarded ?? false}` plus `reason` only when it is a non-empty string; fields kept as sent (`stored: 0`, `forwarded: "yes"`). A non-2xx gives `{ok: false, stored ?? false, forwarded ?? false}` and never a `reason` (the 409's "session-unavailable" is dropped). Non-JSON reads as `{}`; a `null` body or a network failure gives all false. The banner stays up until `dismissFeedback`.
      - Recorder caveat found (not a TS bug): undici's `new Response("", { status: 204 })` throws "Invalid response status code 204", so a recorded 204 reaches the hook as a rejected fetch while the replay feeds it a 204 response. U01i's two `status-set model` 204 steps pass only because both paths keep the pick. The new scenarios use 201. (U01p.)
      - Differences (deliberate, crate convention): typed action arguments (`SendImage { id: Option<String>, ordinal: Option<f64>, data, media_type }`, so a non-string image id can't be passed, which the TS would keep; `reorder_queued(dir: f64)`; `FeedbackInput`). A 503 `error` object that shadows `toString` reads as `""` (the TS throws inside a React updater). Two in-flight calls of one action resolve in fetch-settle order, with no call id.
      - Not replayed (unit-tested): every action without a bound session (`None` / `""`), `requestRecap` included; an SSE title echo landing while a rename is in flight (`act()` answers each fetch within its step); an image-length object in `editQueued`.
      - Tests: `client_session_golden.rs` replays 1561 steps in 99 scenarios. The 88 already in W are byte-identical. The `queue-` block appends 11 scenarios (184 steps): U01n's 2 first, then `queue:updated` snapshots, send runs or queues, send failures, enqueue, cancel / edit / reorder / send now, interrupt and takeOver, renameTitle, setGoal / clearGoal, submitFeedback. Every promise-valued step records `resolved`, and the replay checks it together with the action name. Two recordings are byte-identical, and the `queue-` scenarios are identical before and after the rebase. Plus 8 unit tests in `queue.rs` (crate lib 59 → 70 with U01n).
      - Mutation 118/118 before the rebase (100 in `queue.rs`, 12 in `account.rs`, 6 on the dispatch arms, the `requestRecap` fix and the jump fold), then 12/12 after it. One redundant check found this way was removed (the network-failure early return in `submitFeedback`'s outcome). Flake 20/20, before and after the rebase.

        - Name clashes: gpui `accent` (row / menu hover) = `--panel-2` (`hover:bg-[var(--panel-2)]`, 204 uses). React `--accent` = gpui `primary`. gpui `muted` (a background) = `--panel-2`, while React `--muted` = `muted_foreground`.

        - From `--panel-2`: hovers, secondary, skeleton, title bar (TitleBar.tsx), table head / foot.
        - From `--accent`: ring, links, progress bar, slider, drag border.
        - From `--border`: scrollbar thumb, input border, off switch track. Chart 1–5 = `--series-1…5`.
        - Derived: primary hover = accent/90 (`hover:opacity-90`). The UI has no `:active` styles, so active = hover everywhere.
        - Soft button variants copy React's `bg-red-500/10 text-red-300 hover:bg-red-500/20` pattern: red, amber, emerald, sky for info.
        - Solid fills: danger = red-500 / hover red-600 / white text. Success, warning and info solids are derived (`<hue>-500`, /90 hover); warning text is black.
        - Selected list / table rows = accent/15; selected tab = accent/20; overlay = black/60; chart grid = white/5 (the CartesianGrid literal, also on light themes).
        - Base colors = the UI's text steps: red, emerald, sky, amber, violet 400 / 300; cyan_light = cyan-400, since no lighter cyan is used.
        - `transparent` where React paints nothing: button, list, table, tab, scrollbar track, description-list label. Caret = foreground. Chart bullish / bearish = emerald-400 / red-400 (no candlestick charts in the UI).
        - gpui's theme-file loader caps alpha: `selection` at 30% (`apply` restores 35%, synthwave 40%) and list / table active at 20% (15% is unaffected). `group_box.title.foreground` is a key with no field.
      - Code: the React UI uses Shiki 4.0.2 with one theme, `github-dark-default`, on every palette. Chat blocks paint it on CodeBlock.tsx's `bg-[#0a0a0a]`, the file editor on `var(--background)`. So light and dark share one style here; the mismatch is React's own.
        - gpui-component's syntect backend doesn't use syntect themes: it maps TextMate scopes to 41 highlight names. `code::SYNTAX` gives the 28 names it produces the github-dark-default color of their scope, matched TextMate-style (longest prefix wins).
        - What collapses: `storage.type` (#ff7b72) and `support.type` (#79c0ff) show `entity.name.type`'s #ffa657. `variable.parameter` shows #e6edf3 instead of #ffa657. A theme file has no font-features key, so gpui-component's own code surfaces (TextView code blocks and inline code, the Input code editor, the diff view) take only the family. First: TaskCreate's "already promoted" dedupe never matches a promoted id, so a re-broadcast TaskCreate duplicates its to-do. `[?]` for the user: fix in TS now, or after cutover.
  - [ ] **T08c** **A visible UI bug in the shipping TS, found by U03a. Ask the user before editing (TS outside `rust/`).** `app/globals.css` has an unlayered `* { border-color: var(--border) }`. Unlayered rules beat `@layer utilities`, so every Tailwind border-color class loses to it, and Chromium never shows the composer's drag / bash / focus borders, a `border-red-500`, or a `border-[var(--accent)]`. Only an inline `borderColor` (the `/color` accent) shows. The fix is to move the rule into `@layer base`, as Tailwind v4's preflight does. Then re-record `rust/scripts/theme-golden.mjs`; `claudius-theme`'s drift test and `composer_frame` follow. Check `prompt-color`, the composer specs and `site-screenshots` after.
    — approved 2026-11-22 (user): make the fix (moving the rule into `@layer base`, with the claudius-theme golden re-recorded). Do it after N03a (which owns `app/` and the e2e runner tonight).
  - [ ] **T08b** **A live information leak in the shipping TS, found by D03c. Ask the user before editing (TS outside `rust/`).** `app/api/workspaces/[id]/files/preview/[...path]/route.ts:119` answers with `new Blob([content.buffer as ArrayBuffer])`. `content` is the `Buffer` `fh.readFile()` returned, and `.buffer` is the whole `ArrayBuffer` it is a view onto — for an empty file that is the 64 KiB `allocUnsafeSlow` scratch chunk, so the response body is 65 536 bytes of uninitialized process memory (whatever that memory last held) instead of nothing. The fix is to pass `content`, the view, rather than `content.buffer`. Rust already serves the empty body, so fixing the TS brings them into agreement rather than breaking parity. No spec loads the preview iframe, so no test pins the current bytes.
    — approved 2026-11-22 (user): make the fix (the one-line `content` fix). Do it after N03a (which owns `app/` and the e2e runner tonight).
  - [x] **T08a** **Decided 2026-10-29 (user): keep TS behaviour.** Symlink containment in the ported file routes: `fs/dirs` (D04), `agents` (G05) and `memory/auto` / `memory/rules` (G08) check containment on the path text only and follow symlinks, as TS does. A repo that ships e.g. `.claude/rules/x.md` as a symlink to `~/.zshrc`, plus a forged cross-origin `PATCH`, overwrites that file. Proposed fix (both sides at once): refuse when the resolved target leaves the resolved folder. It breaks rule / agent / memory files that are themselves symlinks into a dotfiles repo, so it needs the user's call.
    — decided 2026-10-29: keep the TS behaviour on both sides (containment on the path text, symlinks followed). No code change; the ported routes keep mirroring TS and list it under PORT_PLAN "Mirrored TS bugs". Revisit only if the TS side changes.
